Austin Debates Airport Art, Housing, & Green Spaces

Thursday, June 5, 2025 Austin City Council Regular Meeting

  • Airport & Downtown Project Spending Under Fire:

    Residents protested millions allocated to out-of-state artists for airport installations and billions for a new convention center, citing lack of transparency, questionable profitability, and neglecting local arts and parks.

  • Environmental Showdown over Water Services:

    Council considered a contentious request to extend water/wastewater to a property outside city limits, sparking debate over protecting endangered species and drinking water from high-intensity development.

  • Student Housing Plan Delayed Amid UT Influence Concerns:

    A crucial vote on increasing West Campus student housing was postponed after last-minute intervention from the University of Texas, drawing criticism for transparency and perceived undue influence.

  • Parks & Public Space Commercialization Questioned:

    A proposed commercial floating dock on Lady Bird Lake was postponed following public outcry over its impact on a tranquil lagoon and the need for proper environmental and community review.

AS EAST AUSTINS COMMUNITIES CONTINUE TO DEVELOP AT A RAPID PACE, THE INCREASING DENSITY OF BUILDINGS, ROADS, AND OTHER INFRASTRUCTURE WILL EXACERBATE A NUMBER OF ISSUES THAT THESE COMMUNITIES ARE DISPROPORTIONATELY VULNERABLE TO AIR AND WA WATER QUALITY, DEGRADATION, ENERGY BURDENS, THE URBAN HEAT ISLAND EFFECT, AND THE INEQUITABLE DISTRIBUTION AND PRESERVATION OF TREES ARE PARTICULARLY OF NOTE. WHILE ALL OF AUSTIN IS EXPECTED TO EXPERIENCE INCREASES IN EXTREME HEAT THROUGH REALITY IS THAT NOT NOT ALL RESIDENTS FACE THE SAME IMPACTS OUTDOOR WORKERS, INFANTS, THE ELDERLY, AND LOW-INCOME COMMUNITIES WITH THE LEAST AMOUNT OF RESOURCES ARE MOST AT RISK. THE REPLACEMENT OF NATURAL VEGETATION WITH BUILDINGS AND PAVED SURFACES INCREASES THE HEAT ISLAND EFFECT, WHICH IF IT'S AVAILABLE, CAN LEAD TO INCREASED ENERGY CONSUMPTION AND CAUSE FOR COOLING HIGHER LEVELS OF AIR POLLUTION AND THE GREATER RISK OF HEAT RELATED ILLNESSES AND MORTALITY. THIS TREND IS DEMONSTRATED BY SATELLITE DATA IN WHICH THE MAJORITY OF AUSTIN'S EASTERN CRESCENT EXPERIENCES HIGHER TEMPERATURES, ALONG WITH INCREASED HEAT RELATED EMS INCIDENTS. IN AN EFFORT TO CONTRIBUTE TOWARD AUSTIN'S NET ZERO GOALS, THE CITY'S ADOPTED CLIMATE EQUITY PLAN HAS ALREADY OUTLINED A NUMBER OF STRATEGIES AS THEY RELATE TO A 50% TREE CANOPY GOAL WITH A FOCUS ON EQUITABLE COVERAGE. THIS UNDERTAKING SHOULD CONTINUE TO BE PRIORITIZED NOT ONLY AS IT RELATES TO CARBON SE SEQUESTRATION, BUT MORE PERTINENTLY TO THE DIRECT AND IMMEDIATE IMPROVEMENTS IN AIR QUALITY AND RELIEF FROM HEAT THAT TREES PROVIDE. SINCE 1928, THE COUNCIL MASTER PLAN CREATED A FORCE RELOCATION OF AFRICAN AMERICANS AND MEXICAN AMERICANS TO EAST AUSTIN. IN 1957, THE INDUSTRIAL DEVELOPMENT ZONING CREATED CUMULATIVE ZONING. RESIDENTIAL HOMES COULD BE BUILT ON LAND ZONE INDUSTRIAL EAST AUSTINITES WERE LIVING NEXT AND ARE ADJACENT TO HAZARDOUS FACILITIES. IN 1998, COUNCIL APPROVED THE NEIGHBORHOOD PLANNING PROCESS AND DIVIDED THE CITY INTO 54 NEIGHBORHOOD PLANNING AREAS. THIS WAS A GENTRIFICATION PROCESS. THE HISTORIC ZONING WAS USED IN EAST AUSTIN TO DRIVE UP THE PRICES OF HOMES, NOT DESIGNATED HISTORIC, ANOTHER GENTRIFICATION TOOL. EAST RIVERSIDE CORRIDOR MASTER PLAN DISPLACED OVER 1,700 LOW INCOME WORKING AND WORKING POOR RESIDENTS. HUNDREDS OF LOWER INCOME UNITS WERE DEMOLISHED. IN 1996, COUNCIL ADOPTED THEIR MOUTH GROWTH INITIATIVES AND DESIRED DEVELOPMENT ZONE. EAST AUSTIN CONTINUES TO BE TARGETED FOR THE INDUSTRIAL POLLUTION BECAUSE THEY BECAME THE DESIRED DEVELOPMENT ZONE. COUNCIL NEEDS TO IMPLEMENT THE PEOPLE'S PLAN. WE OPPOSE CODE NEXT AND HOME AND DB 90 FOR THE FOLLOWING REASON. IT DOESN'T ADHERE TO THE ADOPTED NEIGHBORHOOD PLANS. IT DOESN'T PRESERVE EXISTING LOW INCOME HOUSING. IT DOESN'T PRESERVE EXISTING PUBLIC PARTICIPATION PROCESSES. DENSITY DOESN'T GIVE US LOW INCOME HOUSING OR EVEN AFFORDABILITY. IT DOESN'T ADDRESS RACISM IN THE CITY'S POLICIES AND PROCEDURES. IT WILL DRIVE UP PROPERTY VALUES AND TAXES. THANK YOU. GOOD AFTERNOON, MAYOR AND CITY COUNCIL MEMBERS. MY NAME IS ADRIAN MACIAS. I AM THE YOUTH COORDINATOR FOR PEREZ YOUNG SCHOLARS FOR JUSTICE. MY NAME IS BETSY GREENBERG AND I LIVE IN DISTRICT NINE, LESS THAN TWO BLOCKS NORTH OF THE UNIVERSITY NEIGHBORHOOD OVERLAY. I'M IN FAVOR OF THIS POSTPONEMENT. IN 2004, AFTER TWO YEARS OF HARD WORK AND BIWEEKLY MEETINGS WITH RENTERS, HOMEOWNERS, BUSINESS OWNERS, DEVELOPERS, FRATERNITIES, SORORITIES, THE UNIVERSITY OF TEXAS AND THE CITY STAFF, A PLAN WAS DEVELOPED TO CREATE DENSE HOUSING FOR STUDENTS NEAR THE UNIVERSITY OF TEXAS AND MAINTAIN A HIGH QUALITY OF LIFE ITS SURROUNDING NEIGHBORHOODS. THE RESULT OF THIS COLLABORATIVE PROCESS INVOLVING VARIED INTERESTS WAS THE UNIVERSITY NEIGHBORHOOD OVERLAY KNOWN AS UNO. UNO HAS BEEN EXTREMELY SUCCESSFUL, RESULTING IN 28,000 NEW RESIDENTS WITH AFFORDABLE HOUSING OPTIONS IN AN AREA THAT WAS DESIGNED TO BE CONVENIENT TO THE UNIVERSITY OF TEXAS, PEDESTRIAN FRIENDLY AND SAFE, WHILE STILL RESPECTING THE SURROUNDING NEIGHBORHOODS. RECENTLY, THE CITY COUNCIL PASSED A RESOLUTION DIRECTING STAFF TO MAKE QUALITY OF LIFE IMPROVEMENTS TO UNO AND TO EXPAND THE HOUSING OPPORTUNITIES INSTEAD OF BRINGING STAKEHOLDERS TOGETHER. THAT WAS DONE 20 YEARS EARLIER. THE STAFF HAS ATTEMPTED TO FOLLOW COUNCIL DIRECTION AND CAME UP WITH A PLAN AS A MEMBER OF PAC, THE CONTACT TEAM. FOR THIS PLAN, I ATTENDED STAKEHOLDER MEETINGS. AT THE FIRST ONE, THERE WAS A PRESENTATION BY STAFF ON THE RESOLUTION, AND THEN MONTHS LATER, A PRESENTATION ON THE STAFF PROPOSAL. QUESTIONS WERE ASKED AND FEEDBACK WAS GIVEN, BUT AT LEAST FOR OUR GROUP, IT SEEMED TO FALL ON DEAF EARS. INSTEAD OF HAVING A PLAN WITH BROAD SUPPORT, EVERYONE HAD COMPLAINTS. DEVELOPERS COMPLAINED ABOUT TOWER SPACING. RELIGIOUS ORGANIZATIONS COMPLAINED ABOUT LIMITATIONS ON PARKING GARAGES. THE UNIVERSITY OF TEXAS AND MANY OTHERS DIDN'T LIKE 600 FEET HEIGHT ON THE DRAG. THE UNIVERSITY TENANTS UNION WAS CONCERNED ABOUT TENANT PROTECTIONS THAT WEREN'T INITIALLY INCLUDED. SAFE HORNS COMPLAINED ABOUT THE LACK OF SAFETY PROTECTIONS AND SURROUNDING NEIGHBORS DIDN'T UNDERSTAND WHY UNO WAS BEING EXPANDED SO THAT THE ZONING ON PROPERTIES WOULD INCLUDE BOTH DB UNO AND DBE TODD. FINALLY, THE PLANNING COMMISSION TOOK A STAB AT THE PROPOSAL WITH NO DISCUSSION AT ALL. THEY DECIDED TO DELETE QUALITY OF LIFE IMPROVEMENTS LIKE TOWER SPACING AND GATEKEEPER REQUIREMENTS. IT TOOK REPEATED MOTIONS TO GET SEVEN VOTES, BUT THEY RECOMMENDED A NEW SUBDISTRICT IN HEIGHT MAP. THEY ALSO DECIDED TO VOTE TO VOTE TO EXPAND THE UNO BOUNDARY INTO SURROUNDING NEIGHBORHOODS SAYING IT WOULD GIVE WARNING TO PEOPLE WHO LIVE THERE ABOUT WHERE THE GROWTH WILL BE. IN OTHER WORDS, THEY'RE TELLING US, WE'RE COMING FOR YOUR HOME. NEXT, THE UNIVERSITY NEIGHBORHOOD OVERLAY REVISION NEEDS TO GO BACK TO THE DRAWING BOARD. I'M RELIEVED TO SEE THAT THE UNIVERSITY OF TEXAS ASKED FOR A POSTPONEMENT, AND I'M HOPEFUL THAT THE NEXT ROUND OF POSSIBLE CHANGES WILL INVOLVE MORE THAN [05:15:01] JUST THE LARGEST STAKEHOLDER AND WILL BE A TRUE COLLABORATIVE PROCESS. GOOD EVENING, MAYOR COUNSEL. MY NAME'S RITA THOMPSON. UM, I'VE BEEN A SOCIAL SERVICE PROVIDER IN AUSTIN SINCE 1980, AND THAT HAS AFFORDED ME THE PRIVILEGE TO WORK WITH VARIOUS COMMUNITIES WITH DIFFERENT NEEDS. UM, WE NOW KNOW FOR CERTAIN THAT DENSITY DOES NOT EQUATE WITH AFFORDABILITY. I HAVE ATTENDED AND WATCHED THE PLANNING COMMISSION AND THE CITY COUNCIL WHERE URBANISTS HAVE CLAIMED THAT THEY KNOW THE BEST PRACTICES AND HAVE DISMISSED THE CONCERNS OF NEIGHBORS AND THEIR NEIGHBORHOODS. THIS MADE ME PAUSE. MY PROFESSION HAS A CODE OF ETHICS AND BEST PRACTICES, AND I DECIDED TO GO AHEAD AND DO A CURSORY CHECK, AND I FOUND THAT THE AMERICAN INSTITUTE OF CERTIFIED PLANNERS HAS ALSO A CORE PRINCIPLES AND URBAN ETHICS. THIS PRINCIPLE MANDATES THAT PLANNING DECISION SHOULD BE FAIR AND JUST ENSURING EQUAL ACCESS TO OPPORTUNITIES AND RESOURCES FOR ALL RESIDENTS REGARDLESS OF THEIR BACKGROUND OR SOCIOECONOMIC STATUS.
